Features include a potential editing domain (N-terminal region with β-barrel features), catalytic Rossmann fold, and a C-terminal leucine-binding domain with coiled-coil-like characteristics.<br><br>**Step 3: Evaluate sequence architecture**<br><br>- The N-terminal region (~residues 1-200) shows features consistent with a **CP1 editing domain** (β-barrel-like structure)<br>- The middle region contains the **HIGH-like and KMSKS-like motifs** characteristic of the catalytic Rossmann fold<br>- The C-terminal region (~residues 380-470) shows alternating charged/hydrophobic patterns consistent with a **connective peptide and amino acid-binding domain** seen in LeuRS<br>- The sequence length (~470 residues) is consistent with a non-editing form of LeuRS<br>- The presence of &quot;RHIEST&quot; after KMSKS resembles the second HIGH-like element found in LeuRS catalytic sites<br><br>**Step 4: Conclusion**<br><br>The combination of Class I motifs, sequence architecture, domain organization, and length most strongly supports this being a **Leucine-tRNA ligase (LeuRS)**.<br><br>## Answer: C</td>
